```text
  /**
   * Write a plan the server derived from a plan file. Unlike a provider update
   * this carries no worker lease: derivation is a property of the objective and
   * the file it names, not of whichever agent happens to hold the session, so
   * it stays valid across worker restarts and reattaches. The revision fence
   * still applies, so a concurrent provider update wins and the derivation is
   * simply refused.
   */
  async applyDerivedPlan(input: DerivedGoalPlanRequest): Promise<GoalMutationResult> {
    return retryGoalBusy(() => this.applyDerivedPlanOnce(input));
  }

  private async applyDerivedPlanOnce(input: DerivedGoalPlanRequest): Promise<GoalMutationResult> {
    const transaction = await this.client.transaction("write");
    try {
      const current = await selectGoal(transaction, input.runId);
      if (!current) {
        await transaction.commit();
        return goalFailure("not_found", "The goal does not exist.", null);
      }
      if (current.status === "cleared") {
        await transaction.commit();
        return goalFailure("invalid_transition", "A cleared goal cannot be updated.", current);
      }
      const conflict = this.validateMutationFence(current, input);
      if (conflict) {
        await transaction.commit();
        return conflict;
      }
      if (
        JSON.stringify(input.plan) === JSON.stringify(current.plan)
        && JSON.stringify(input.planSource) === JSON.stringify(current.planSource)
      ) {
        await transaction.commit();
        return { ok: true, snapshot: current, replayed: true };
      }
      const now = this.now().getTime();
      const revision = current.revision + 1;
      await transaction.execute({
        sql: `UPDATE run_goals SET plan_json = ?, plan_source_json = ?, revision = ?,
                transition_source = 'reconciliation', updated_at = ?
              WHERE run_id = ? AND goal_id = ? AND revision = ? AND status != 'cleared'`,
        args: [
          JSON.stringify(input.plan),
          JSON.stringify(input.planSource),
          revision,
          now,
          input.runId,
          input.goalId,
          input.expectedRevision,
        ],
      });
      const snapshot = await selectGoal(transaction, input.runId);
      if (!snapshot || snapshot.revision !== revision) {
        await transaction.rollback();
        return goalFailure("revision_conflict", "The goal changed before the derived plan was committed.", snapshot);
      }
      await enqueueGoalEvent(transaction, snapshot, "goal.plan.updated", now, this.randomId);
      await transaction.commit();
      return { ok: true, snapshot, replayed: false };
    } catch (error) {
      await transaction.rollback();
      throw error;
    }
  }

  async isControlSettled(snapshot: GoalSnapshot) {
    const result = await this.client.execute({
      sql: `SELECT control_method FROM run_goals
            WHERE run_id = ? AND goal_id = ? AND revision = ? AND lease_generation = ?
              AND worker_id = ? AND acp_session_id = ? LIMIT 1`,
      args: [
        snapshot.runId,
        snapshot.goalId,
        snapshot.revision,
        snapshot.leaseGeneration,
        snapshot.workerId,
        snapshot.acpSessionId,
      ],
    });
    return typeof result.rows[0]?.control_method === "string" && Boolean(result.rows[0].control_method);
  }

  async markControlApplied(snapshot: GoalSnapshot, method: GoalControlMethod, action: GoalMutationAction = "set") {
    const marker = `${method}:${snapshot.acpSessionId}:${snapshot.leaseGeneration}:${snapshot.revision}`;
    const result = await this.client.execute({
      sql: `UPDATE run_goals SET control_method = ?, updated_at = ?
            WHERE run_id = ? AND goal_id = ? AND revision = ? AND lease_generation = ?
              AND worker_id = ? AND acp_session_id = ? AND control_method IS NULL
              AND (status != 'cleared' OR ? = 'clear')`,
      args: [
        marker,
        this.now().getTime(),
        snapshot.runId,
        snapshot.goalId,
        snapshot.revision,
        snapshot.leaseGeneration,
        snapshot.workerId,
        snapshot.acpSessionId,
        action,
      ],
    });
    return result.rowsAffected === 1;
  }

  async recordControlFailure(
    snapshot: GoalSnapshot,
    kind: "unsupported" | "transport",
    message: string,
  ): Promise<GoalMutationResult> {
    const safeMessage = redactGoalErrorMessage(message);
    return retryGoalBusy(() => this.recordControlFailureOnce(snapshot, kind, safeMessage));
  }

  private async recordControlFailureOnce(
    snapshot: GoalSnapshot,
    kind: "unsupported" | "transport",
    message: string,
  ): Promise<GoalMutationResult> {
    const transaction = await this.client.transaction("write");
    try {
      const current = await selectGoal(transaction, snapshot.runId);
      if (!current) {
        await transaction.commit();
        return goalFailure("not_found", "The goal does not exist.", null);
      }
      if (
        current.goalId !== snapshot.goalId
        || current.revision !== snapshot.revision
        || current.leaseGeneration !== snapshot.leaseGeneration
        || current.workerId !== snapshot.workerId
        || current.acpSessionId !== snapshot.acpSessionId
      ) {
        await transaction.commit();
        return goalFailure("stale_lease", "The failed control belongs to a stale goal lease.", current);
      }
      if (current.status === "cleared") {
        await transaction.commit();
        return goalFailure("invalid_transition", "A cleared goal cannot record a control failure.", current);
      }
      const nextStatus: GoalStatus = kind === "unsupported" ? "limited" : "error";
      if (!canTransitionGoalStatus(current.status, nextStatus)) {
        await transaction.commit();
        return goalFailure("invalid_transition", `Cannot mark a ${current.status} goal as ${nextStatus}.`, current);
      }
      const revision = current.revision + 1;
      const now = this.now().getTime();
      await transaction.execute({
        sql: `UPDATE run_goals SET status = ?, revision = ?, last_error = ?, control_method = ?,
                transition_source = 'reconciliation', updated_at = ?
              WHERE run_id = ? AND goal_id = ? AND revision = ? AND lease_generation = ?
                AND worker_id = ? AND acp_session_id = ? AND status != 'cleared'`,
        args: [
          nextStatus,
          revision,
          message,
          kind,
          now,
          snapshot.runId,
          snapshot.goalId,
          snapshot.revision,
          snapshot.leaseGeneration,
          snapshot.workerId,
          snapshot.acpSessionId,
        ],
      });
      const next = await selectGoal(transaction, snapshot.runId);
      if (!next || next.revision !== revision) {
        await transaction.rollback();
        return goalFailure("stale_lease", "The goal changed before the control failure was recorded.", next);
      }
      await enqueueGoalEvent(transaction, next, kind === "unsupported" ? "goal.limited" : "goal.updated", now, this.randomId);
      await transaction.commit();
      return { ok: true, snapshot: next, replayed: false };
    } catch (error) {
      await transaction.rollback();
      throw error;
    }
  }

  async finalizeOperation(runId: string, operationId: string, result: GoalMutationResult) {
    return finalizeGoalOperation(this.client, this.now, runId, operationId, result);
  }

  private validateMutationFence(current: GoalSnapshot, input: Pick<OperationIdentity, "goalId" | "expectedRevision">) {
    if (current.goalId !== input.goalId || current.revision !== input.expectedRevision) {
      return goalFailure("revision_conflict", "The goal revision is stale.", current);
    }
    return null;
  }
}
export function createGoalControlService(client: DbClient, options: ServiceOptions = {}) {
  return new GoalControlService(client, options);
}

export const goalControl = createGoalControlService(dbClient);
```

## Claude thinking · 2026-10-14T13:24:41.363Z

I realize applyProviderUpdate doesn't reset control_method, so the revision bump alone doesn't clear the settled marker — meaning isControlSettled would still see it as true and report already_applied even after a fresh dispatch, which isn't the behavior I want.

So I need a dedicated method, something like parkGoalOnUser, that properly transitions pursuing to waiting_user with control_method set to NULL, tags the transition source as reconciliation, records the error reason, and queues the right event kind for the frontend. Then I still need coordinator.dispatch to actually send the clear command afterward, and I need to work out what recoveryAction should return for waiting_user goals without breaking existing Codex behavior — particularly figuring out what provider state makes sense when a Codex-reported waiting_user goal needs reconciliation.

I'm also noticing a related bug: resending "set" re-arms the goal, which is exactly the problem we're trying to avoid for Claude, and the same issue could occur if a session gets recreated — initializeWorkerGoalSession dispatches "set" unconditionally rather than checking recoveryAction, even for paused goals. That seems like it should really call recoveryAction(snapshot) instead, though it's a pre-existing pattern that's somewhat out of scope for this fix.

For waiting_user specifically, dispatching "pause" on a fresh session with no established goal seems risky since there's nothing to pause yet, so maybe the safest approach is skipping dispatch entirely in that case and letting control_method stay unset, since the next startup recovery would just send "clear" — which is harmless on a goal-less session. I'm trying to work out the right semantics: whether waiting_user should map to "clear" only when there's no extension-based pause capability, but the snapshot doesn't expose enough to tell, so I'd need to check something like capabilities.fallbackMethod existing without a pause capability instead.

I'm settling on treating waiting_user as a parked goal — recoveryAction should return "clear" if there's no pause capability, otherwise "pause", and initializeWorkerGoalSession should call recoveryAction only for waiting_user status while leaving other statuses to use "set" as before. This feels consistent with "don't actively pursue a goal that's waiting on the user," and later a user-triggered retry would still work since it dispatches a fresh "set" action that re-arms the goal properly.

Now I'm also considering whether a regular user message should auto re-arm a waiting_user goal — but since this session's earlier messages were unrelated bug reports, auto re-arming would recreate the same loop problem, so I'll leave that manual via the Resume button. I also want to make sure goal-worker-continuation logic only targets pursuing goals, excluding waiting_user, and that I only act on Stop hook triggers when there's a pursuing goal whose lease belongs to this worker/session — otherwise a user's own buggy Stop hook could cause unintended behavior.

I'm weighing whether to suppress the raw warning text shown in chat, or just leave the transcript as-is since the loop stops after the first cap anyway. Lowering the stop-hook block cap for Claude workers could reduce spam, but that risks undermining the goal feature's core behavior of legitimately continuing when the goal isn't yet met, so I need to be careful not to break that.

I'm checking whether the block counter actually resets when the agent performs real tool calls between stop attempts, but the code isn't clear on this, so I'll leave that mechanism untouched. I also want to check whether "goal.blocked" is a handled event kind or if I should just use "goal.updated" instead, and whether GoalPlanCard actually surfaces lastError to the user.

```text
  publishClaim(claim: GoalOutboxClaim) {
    const payload = JSON.parse(claim.payloadJson) as Record<string, unknown>;
    const snapshot = { ...payload };
    delete snapshot.eventKey;
    this.emit({
      kind: claim.eventKind,
      eventKey: claim.eventKey,
      runId: claim.runId,
      goalId: claim.goalId,
      revision: claim.revision,
      leaseGeneration: claim.leaseGeneration,
      snapshot,
    } as NamedEvent);
  }

  async drainOnce(): Promise<GoalOutboxDrainResult> {
    const claim = await this.claimNext();
    if (!claim) return "idle";
    try {
      this.publishClaim(claim);
      const acknowledged = await this.acknowledge(claim);
      return acknowledged ? "published" : "stale_claim";
    } catch (error) {
      return this.recordFailure(claim, error);
    }
  }

  async drainPending(limit = 100) {
    let publishedCount = 0;
    let poisonedCount = 0;
    try {
      for (let index = 0; index < limit; index += 1) {
        const result = await this.drainOnce();
        if (result === "idle") break;
        if (result === "published") publishedCount += 1;
        if (result === "poisoned") poisonedCount += 1;
        if (result === "retryable" || result === "stale_claim") break;
      }
      return { publishedCount, poisonedCount };
    } finally {
      if (this.autoDeliveryEnabled) await this.scheduleNextAutoDelivery();
    }
  }

  async startAutoDelivery(limit = 1_000) {
    this.autoDeliveryEnabled = true;
    return this.drainPending(limit);
  }

  stopAutoDelivery() {
    this.autoDeliveryEnabled = false;
```

## Claude thinking · 2026-10-14T13:25:10.099Z

I'm weighing whether "goal.blocked" fits semantically, but since the remaining items depend on user input rather than being truly blocked, "waiting_user" status paired with a generic "goal.updated" event seems more accurate. Now I need to figure out where in runtime-client.sessionUpdate to actually detect this condition.

I'm checking before the message chunk handling and considering adding a check for the goal stop-hook cap notice that triggers parking the goal. Since sessionUpdate is awaited by the ACP client, I'm thinking through whether dispatching `/goal clear` mid-turn would hit a worker_busy state and defer, and whether an HTTP call back to the same process (since the bridge and runtime-client appear to run in the same process) could risk deadlocking during sessionUpdate processing — though async HTTP calls to self should be fine in Node.

To avoid deadlock risk from sequential notification processing, I'm leaning toward a fire-and-forget dispatch with error handling rather than awaiting the clear directly. There's also a race concern: if the turn settles before the deferred goal control is parked, it might not retry until the next turn settles, so I want to check where retryDeferredGoalControl is actually invoked to confirm this doesn't leave the goal stuck armed.

## Claude thinking · 2026-10-14T13:25:20.445Z

Since another agent is concurrently editing sync.ts with uncommitted changes, I'll steer clear of that file entirely. I'm also tracing a potential race condition: the retry logic for deferred goal control only fires when the turn transitions to awaiting_user/done, but if the cap triggers right as the turn ends, the deferred control might get parked asynchronously after the turn-ending notification already went out, creating a timing gap around the warning chunk.

I'm also considering whether askAgent could fail with a busy error if dispatch happens while the agent is still finishing up, versus being dispatched cleanly once idle. Rather than moving the dispatch logic to run on turn settle, I think it's safer to keep it in the sessionUpdate handler but rely on the deferral machinery as a backup, plus add startup recovery for any NULL control state. I still need to check how dispatchSlash handles the "clear" case through askAgent to confirm the busy-error behavior.

```text
      if (!isAgentBusyError(error)) throw error;
      return { kind: "deferred", reason: "worker_busy" };
    } finally {
      clearTimeout(timer);
    }
    return { kind: "dispatched", method: "extension" };
  }

  private async dispatchSlash(snapshot: GoalSnapshot, action: GoalMutationAction): Promise<GoalAcpDispatchResult> {
    try {
      // The ask stream stays open until the turn `/goal` started ends, so the
      // dispatch settles once the runtime has taken the prompt.
      let onAccepted: () => void = () => {};
      const acceptance = new Promise<void>((resolve) => {
        onAccepted = resolve;
      });
      const request = this.dependencies.sendSlashCommand(snapshot.workerId!, fallbackCommand(snapshot, action), {
        onAccepted: () => onAccepted(),
      });
      await this.untilAccepted(snapshot, action, "slash", request, acceptance);
    } catch (error) {
      // The slash fallback is a prompt, and a prompt cannot start while the
      // agent is mid-turn. That is a "not yet", not a broken transport: burning
      // it into `error` left the goal dead for the rest of the session, and
      // every retry the user pressed while the turn ran repeated the same
      // failure. Defer instead; the turn-settled reconciliation re-dispatches.
      if (!isAgentBusyError(error)) throw error;
      return { kind: "deferred", reason: "worker_busy" };
    }
    return { kind: "dispatched", method: "slash" };
  }

  /**
   * Resolve when `request` settles or the agent has accepted it, whichever
   * comes first. A request still running after acceptance keeps running with
   * the agent's turn; if it later fails, that is reported, not rethrown.
   */
  private async untilAccepted(
    snapshot: GoalSnapshot,
    action: GoalMutationAction,
    method: "extension" | "slash",
    request: Promise<unknown>,
    acceptance: Promise<void>,
  ) {
    let accepted = false;
    const acceptedFirst = acceptance.then(() => {
      accepted = true;
    });
    request.catch((error: unknown) => {
      if (!accepted) return;
      emitNamedEvent({
        kind: "goal.control.failed_after_acceptance",
        runId: snapshot.runId,
        goalId: snapshot.goalId,
        workerId: snapshot.workerId!,
        action,
        method,
        reason: redactGoalErrorMessage(error),
      });
    });
    await Promise.race([request, acceptedFirst]);
  }
}

export function createGoalAcpDispatcher(dependencies: GoalAcpDependencies = defaultDependencies) {
  return new GoalAcpDispatcher(dependencies);
}

export const goalAcpDispatcher = createGoalAcpDispatcher();
```

## Claude thinking · 2026-10-14T13:25:46.300Z

I'm checking whether sending `/goal clear` through askAgent would actually surface a "Goal cleared" message in the conversation stream, which seems like acceptable, informative behavior. I'm also wondering how a slash command gets handled if the runtime queue is busy rather than erroring out.

I want to make sure the cap warning only triggers for the correct worker/session that's actively pursuing the current goal lease, verifying it checks `workerId`, `acpSessionId`, and status match. I'm also noting that some repeated "Still blocked" spam before the first cap hit is probably unavoidable without lowering the cap threshold, and now I'm second-guessing whether stopping the goal right at the first cap hit is actually the correct behavior.

I'm designing a new `markWaitingForUser` method on GoalControlService that parks a pursued goal back on the user once the agent stalls out after repeated stop attempts. It runs inside a transaction, validates the goal's fencing tokens, confirms the pursuing→waiting_user transition is legal, updates the row with the new status and cleared control method, and enqueues a goal-updated event before committing, using the busy-retry helper. Then I'm working through what recovery action should follow for a waiting_user goal depending on whether the provider supports pause capabilities.

Since pause isn't supported for Claude, I verify the recovery resolves to "clear" and trace through the dispatcher logic to confirm that path correctly falls back to the slash-command dispatch when the extension metadata is absent. I decide to route through `recoveryAction(parked)` rather than hardcoding "clear" so pause-capable agents still get paused appropriately, and I'm adjusting the worker goal session initializer so that when a snapshot is already in waiting_user status it uses recoveryAction instead of defaulting to "set", leaving other statuses like paused untouched.

I'm checking whether this change actually matters: in the scenario where a worker session is lost and a new worker attaches, without this fix the goal would get re-armed via "set" even though we're waiting on user input, causing a repeated loop next turn. Sending "clear" on a brand-new session is harmless since there's no goal to clear, and it also prevents permanent dispatch retries on restart; for resumed sessions with the same worker and session id, the control method is preserved so no redundant dispatch occurs, and since the transcript already reflects the clear, the goal state stays correctly cleared after resume. I'm also noting that transitioning from waiting_user back to pursuing via a "retry" action should set pursuing state, null out control, and dispatch the retry action appropriately.

Now I'm sketching a new detection utility that scans agent message chunks for a regex matching the "hook blocked the turn from ending N consecutive times" notice, and planning a handler, `parkGoalAfterStopHookCap`, that would resolve the run id for a worker and park the goal state accordingly, likely placed alongside the existing goal-state module so I can mirror its dependency-injection test style.

```text
import { beforeEach, describe, expect, it, vi } from "vitest";

  askAgent: vi.fn(),
}));

vi.mock("@/server/bridge-client", () => bridgeMocks);

describe("goal worker reconciliation", () => {
  beforeEach(async () => {
    await db.delete(runGoalOutbox);
    await db.delete(runGoalOperations);
    await db.delete(runGoals);
    await db.delete(workers);
    await db.delete(runs);
    await db.delete(plans);
    const now = Date.now();
    await dbClient.batch([
      { sql: "INSERT INTO plans (id, path, status, created_at, updated_at) VALUES (?, ?, ?, ?, ?)", args: ["goal-worker-plan", "/tmp/goal-worker.md", "running", now, now] },
      { sql: "INSERT INTO runs (id, plan_id, status, created_at, updated_at) VALUES (?, ?, ?, ?, ?)", args: ["goal-worker-run", "goal-worker-plan", "running", now, now] },
      { sql: "INSERT INTO workers (id, run_id, type, status, cwd, created_at, updated_at) VALUES (?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?)", args: ["goal-worker", "goal-worker-run", "codex", "idle", "/tmp", now, now] },
    ], "write");
    await goalControl.putGoal({
      runId: "goal-worker-run",
      goalId: "goal-1",
      expectedRevision: 0,
      operationId: "goal-worker-op",
      principalId: "test",
      endpoint: "goal.put",
      objective: "Reconcile the worker",
    });
    __resetAdvertisedCommandsForTests();
    bridgeMocks.getAgent.mockReset();
    bridgeMocks.invokeAgentAcpMethod.mockReset();
    bridgeMocks.askAgent.mockReset();
  });

  it("attaches and applies a durable goal only once for one worker session revision", async () => {
    const dispatch = vi.fn(async () => ({ kind: "dispatched" as const, method: "extension" as const }));
    const first = await initializeWorkerGoalSession("goal-worker", "session-1", { dispatch });
    const second = await initializeWorkerGoalSession("goal-worker", "session-1", { dispatch });

    expect(first).toMatchObject({ kind: "accepted", snapshot: { revision: 2, leaseGeneration: 1 } });
    expect(second).toMatchObject({ kind: "accepted", snapshot: { revision: 2, leaseGeneration: 1 } });
    expect(dispatch).toHaveBeenCalledTimes(1);
  });

  it("restores advertised controls when reattaching before a new goal frame", async () => {
    const dispatch = vi.fn(async () => ({ kind: "dispatched" as const, method: "extension" as const }));
    await initializeWorkerGoalSession("goal-worker", "session-reattach", {
      dispatch,
      agentCapabilities: { _meta: { goal: { version: 1, actions: ["set", "pause", "resume", "clear"] } } },
    });
    expect(await goalControl.getGoal("goal-worker-run")).toMatchObject({
      capabilities: { set: true, edit: true, pause: true, resume: true, clear: true },
    });
  });

  it("replays a committed but undispatched control during startup recovery", async () => {
    const attached = await goalControl.attachLease({
      runId: "goal-worker-run",
      goalId: "goal-1",
      expectedRevision: 1,
      workerId: "goal-worker",
      acpSessionId: "session-1",
    });
    if (!attached.ok) throw new Error(attached.message);
    bridgeMocks.getAgent.mockResolvedValue({
      agentCapabilities: { _meta: { goal: { version: 1, capabilities: { set: true } } } },
    });
    bridgeMocks.invokeAgentAcpMethod.mockResolvedValue({ ok: true });

    await recoverPendingGoalControlsAtStartup();

    expect(bridgeMocks.invokeAgentAcpMethod).toHaveBeenCalledWith(
      "goal-worker",
      "_session/goal",
      expect.objectContaining({
        sessionId: "session-1",
        goalId: "goal-1",
        revision: 2,
        action: "set",
        objective: "Reconcile the worker",
      }),
    );
    expect(await goalControl.isControlSettled(attached.snapshot)).toBe(true);
  });

  it("accepts normalized metadata and plan updates only from the current lease", async () => {
    const dispatch = vi.fn(async () => ({ kind: "dispatched" as const, method: "extension" as const }));
    await initializeWorkerGoalSession("goal-worker", "session-1", { dispatch });
    const metadata = await handleAcpGoalSessionUpdateForWorker({
      workerId: "goal-worker",
      sessionId: "session-1",
      update: {
        sessionUpdate: "session_info",
        _meta: { goal: { version: 1, status: "in_progress", capabilities: { pause: true, resume: true, clear: true } } },
      },
    });
    expect(metadata).toMatchObject({ kind: "accepted", revision: 3 });

    const fallback = await handleAcpGoalSessionUpdateForWorker({
      workerId: "goal-worker",
      sessionId: "session-1",
      update: {
        sessionUpdate: "available_commands_update",
        availableCommands: [{ name: "goal" }, { name: "goal pause" }, { name: "goal resume" }],
      },
    });
    expect(fallback).toMatchObject({ kind: "accepted", revision: 4 });

    const plan = await handleAcpGoalSessionUpdateForWorker({
      workerId: "goal-worker",
      sessionId: "session-1",
      update: { sessionUpdate: "plan", entries: [{ content: "Inspect", status: "in_progress" }] },
    });
    expect(plan).toMatchObject({ kind: "accepted", revision: 5 });
    expect(await goalControl.getGoal("goal-worker-run")).toMatchObject({
      status: "pursuing",
      plan: [{ title: "Inspect", status: "in_progress" }],
    });

    await initializeWorkerGoalSession("goal-worker", "session-2", { dispatch });
    const stale = await handleAcpGoalSessionUpdateForWorker({
      workerId: "goal-worker",
      sessionId: "session-1",
      update: { sessionUpdate: "plan", entries: [{ content: "Stale", status: "completed" }] },
    });
```

Good, tests use a real DB, so I'll create a new test file following this style for the goal-stop-hook-cap scenario. I need to confirm how the goal status moves from pending to pursuing after the slash set dispatch, since markControlApplied doesn't change status—for this case the goal is already pursuing at revision 36, so that's fine. Now I'll start implementing, beginning with GoalControlService.markWaitingForUser and then the goal-state logic.

I should export `isGoalStopHookCapNotice` and `handleGoalStopHookCapForWorker`, then wire the runtime client to call it non-blockingly, followed by the recovery action and worker goal session initialization. For named events, I'm considering adding a typed `goal.waiting_user` event with the run, goal, worker IDs and a stop_hook_block_cap reason, plus an ignored variant like `goal.stop_hook_cap.ignored` for cases where there's no goal, it's not pursuing, or the lease is stale—I want to check whether just adding to the union type is sufficient or if there's a separate registry/validation list I need to update too.

```json
  "new_string": "  /**\n   * Park a pursued goal on the user once the agent has stopped pursuing it.\n   *\n   * Control is left unsettled on purpose: the dispatch that follows disarms the\n   * goal in the agent, which would otherwise keep re-arming it on every turn.\n   */\n  async markWaitingForUser(snapshot: GoalSnapshot): Promise<GoalMutationResult> {\n    return retryGoalBusy(() => this.markWaitingForUserOnce(snapshot));\n  }\n\n  private async markWaitingForUserOnce(snapshot: GoalSnapshot): Promise<GoalMutationResult> {\n    const transaction = await this.client.transaction(\"write\");\n    try {\n      const current = await selectGoal(transaction, snapshot.runId);\n      if (!current) {\n        await transaction.commit();\n        return goalFailure(\"not_found\", \"The goal does not exist.\", null);\n      }\n      if (\n        current.goalId !== snapshot.goalId\n        || current.revision !== snapshot.revision\n        || current.leaseGeneration !== snapshot.leaseGeneration\n        || current.workerId !== snapshot.workerId\n        || current.acpSessionId !== snapshot.acpSessionId\n      ) {\n        await transaction.commit();\n        return goalFailure(\"stale_lease\", \"The goal changed before it could wait for the user.\", current);\n      }\n      if (current.status !== \"pursuing\") {\n        await transaction.commit();\n        return goalFailure(\"invalid_transition\", `Cannot park a ${current.status} goal on the user.`, current);\n      }\n      const revision = current.revision + 1;\n      const now = this.now().getTime();\n      await transaction.execute({\n        sql: `UPDATE run_goals SET status = 'waiting_user', revision = ?, control_method = NULL,\n                transition_source = 'reconciliation', updated_at = ?\n              WHERE run_id = ? AND goal_id = ? AND revision = ? AND lease_generation = ?\n                AND worker_id = ? AND acp_session_id = ? AND status = 'pursuing'`,\n        args: [\n          revision,\n          now,\n          snapshot.runId,\n          snapshot.goalId,\n          snapshot.revision,\n          snapshot.leaseGeneration,\n          snapshot.workerId,\n          snapshot.acpSessionId,\n        ],\n      });\n      const next = await selectGoal(transaction, snapshot.runId);\n      if (!next || next.revision !== revision) {\n        await transaction.rollback();\n        return goalFailure(\"stale_lease\", \"The goal changed before it could wait for the user.\", next);\n      }\n      await enqueueGoalEvent(transaction, next, \"goal.updated\", now, this.randomId);\n      await transaction.commit();\n      return { ok: true, snapshot: next, replayed: false };\n    } catch (error) {\n      await transaction.rollback();\n      throw error;\n    }\n  }\n\n  async recordControlFailure(\n    snapshot: GoalSnapshot,"
}
```

```json
  "old_string": "export function recoveryAction(snapshot: GoalSnapshot): GoalMutationAction {\n  if (snapshot.status === \"cleared\") return \"clear\";\n  if (snapshot.status === \"paused\") return \"pause\";\n  return \"set\";\n}",
  "new_string": "export function recoveryAction(snapshot: GoalSnapshot): GoalMutationAction {\n  if (snapshot.status === \"cleared\") return \"clear\";\n  if (snapshot.status === \"paused\") return \"pause\";\n  // A goal waiting on the user must not be pursued by the agent. Re-setting it\n  // re-armed Claude's `/goal` Stop hook, and every later turn then ended with\n  // the agent repeating that it was blocked until the hook cap overrode it.\n  // Claude cannot pause a goal, so clearing it in the agent is the only way to\n  // stand it down; resuming sends the objective again.\n  if (snapshot.status === \"waiting_user\") return snapshot.capabilities.pause ? \"pause\" : \"clear\";\n  return \"set\";\n}"
}
```

## Claude thinking · 2026-10-14T13:26:33.581Z

I'm adding a detection regex for Claude Code's notice about overriding a Stop hook after repeated blocks, and setting up the handler in goal-state.ts to accept an optional dispatch parameter for testability, mirroring initializeWorkerGoalSession.

Writing park logic...

After checking the lease still matches, I mark the goal as waiting for user and dispatch a disarm recovery action, mirroring how retryDeferredGoalControl handles outcomes but emitting a reconciliation.refused event instead of recording failure when clear is unsupported, and logging transport errors as reconciliation.failed. I'm now weighing whether the dispatch call inside the sessionUpdate path should be awaited, since it's invoked from the runtime-client.

There's a race to consider: the dispatch happens while the turn is still finishing, so askAgent might return "busy" and get deferred into coordinator.deferredByRun, to be retried once sync.ts sees the run settle into awaiting_user/done — this should be fine given the handler's DB operations are fast. I'm also trusting that the existing goal-set code reliably surfaces the busy error rather than having the bridge silently queue the prompt, and noting that `/goal clear` isn't a startsTurn slash command, so dispatchSlash just awaits acceptance via untilAccepted.

Looking at the coordinator flow: converge calls isControlSettled, sees control_method is NULL, dispatches, then calls markControlApplied with "slash"/"clear" to set the marker — and Claude's goal_status handler emits the "Goal cleared" text into the stream, with no AIR-only publish issue to worry about. Now I'm adding the clear-handling code in goal-state.ts right after handleAcpGoalSessionUpdateForWorker.

When what is left needs the user (a\n * device, a recording, a decision), the agent can only answer \"still blocked\"\n * until Claude Code gives up after its block cap. The goal stays armed, so every\n * later turn (each user message, each background task notification) replayed\n * the same run of \"still blocked\" replies ending in the cap warning. The cap is\n * the signal that the agent has stopped pursuing the goal, so the goal moves to\n * `waiting_user` and is disarmed in the agent until the user resumes it.\n */\nexport async function parkGoalAfterStopHookBlockCap(args: {\n  workerId: string;\n  sessionId: string | null | undefined;\n  dispatch?: (\n    snapshot: import(\"@/shared/goal-plan\").GoalSnapshot,\n    action: import(\"@/shared/goal-plan\").GoalMutationAction,\n  ) => Promise<{ kind: string; reason?: string }>;\n}): Promise<GoalStopHookCapResult> {\n  const { emitNamedEvent } = await import(\"@/server/events/named-events\");\n  const runId = await resolveRunIdForGoalWorker(args.workerId);\n  const skip = (reason: string, goalId: string | null = null): GoalStopHookCapResult => {\n    emitNamedEvent({ kind: \"goal.park_skipped\", runId, goalId, workerId: args.workerId, reason });\n    return { kind: \"ignored\", reason };\n  };\n  if (!runId) return skip(\"worker_not_persisted\");\n  const { goalControl } = await import(\"@/server/runs/goal-control\");\n  const current = await goalControl.getGoal(runId);\n  if (!current || !current.visible || current.status === \"cleared\") return skip(\"goal_absent\");\n  if (current.status !== \"pursuing\") return skip(`goal_${current.status}`, current.goalId);\n  const sessionId = typeof args.sessionId === \"string\" ? <!-- codex-event:{"kind":"reasoning","timestamp":"2026-10-14T13:27:13.535Z","phase":null} -->
## Claude thinking · 2026-10-14T13:27:13.535Z

The worker-based DB access looks fine since similar handling already exists there. Now I'm planning tests for the stop-hook cap notice: verifying the detection function matches the real text while ignoring unrelated chunks, and confirming a pursuing goal held by the worker's session gets parked on the user and disarmed when capability-limited behavior is detected.

I also need tests covering ignored notices from stale sessions or non-pursuing goals, correct recovery actions for waiting_user status with and without pause, and verifying that initializing a worker session with an existing waiting_user goal dispatches "clear" rather than "set" — but I notice the dispatch coordinator only passes the snapshot, dropping the action, so I'll need to route through bridge mocks and check that askAgent gets called with the clear command via the fallback slash path instead.

## Claude thinking · 2026-10-14T13:28:28.366Z

I need to fix the session without restarting the runner, since that would disrupt other active agent sessions. The goal API doesn't have a clean "waiting_user" action, so I'm weighing whether to just send a `/goal clear` directly as a workaround.

Actually, maybe I should edit the DB row directly to set it to waiting_user, since the runner treats the DB as the source of truth for goal control — the UI snapshot would eventually pick up the change even without an outbox event firing immediately. I'm checking whether worker-2 is idle and considering whether there's an HTTP route on the bridge (port 7800) I could hit to message the worker directly, but running my new code out-of-process raises complications since the outbox events would get drained into nowhere rather than the runner's actual SSE stream.

Calling my function directly seems cleanest, but I need to verify the worker_id and session_id match what's in run_goals, and confirm how bridge-client resolves its URL and auth token when run from a standalone script.

## Claude · 2026-10-14T13:29:14.421Z

Session fc8d9cb681ef is fixed, and the code now handles this case so it won't keep happening. The runner is still on the old code until it restarts (see the end).

## What was going on

The conversation has a Claude `/goal` running: "complete the three cloudcaptions plans". Claude Code runs a goal as a Stop hook. Every time the agent tries to end a turn, a separate checker decides whether the goal is done, and blocks the stop if it isn't.

What's left in those plans needs things only you can provide: the Pixel run, the 10- and 30-minute recordings, labels, the assistant billing decision, and rollout approval. The checker said so itself, but it still returned "not met" instead of "can't be done". So at the end of every turn:

- the agent answered "Still blocked on your inputs." about 8 times in a row;
- Claude Code then hit its limit of 9 blocked stops, forced the turn to end, and printed that warning.

The goal stayed active after that. Every later turn ran the same loop: each of your messages, and each notification from a finished background task (that's what started the 11:18 turn today, with no message from you). That's why you saw the warning 6 times.

On the OmniHarness side, nothing ever moved a Claude goal out of `pursuing`. The goal card already has a "Waiting for you" status with a Resume button, but no server code ever set that status.

## Code fix

- **Spotting the warning** (`src/server/agent-runtime/acp/goal-state.ts`, called from `runtime-client.ts`): when Claude's "hook blocked the turn from ending N consecutive times" warning arrives from the worker that holds a pursued goal, OmniHarness now:
  - moves the goal to `waiting_user`;
  - sends `/goal clear` to Claude, since Claude has no pause, so later turns end normally;
  - if the agent is still finishing its turn, sends the clear once the turn ends.
- **New status change** (`goal-control.ts`, `markWaitingForUser`): a guarded `pursuing` → `waiting_user` transition.
- **No re-arming** (`goal-control-dispatch.ts`, `goal-state.ts`): startup recovery and a new session attaching no longer send the objective again for a goal that's waiting on you. Before, a session restart would have restarted the loop.
- **Resuming**: pressing Resume on the goal card sends `/goal <objective>` again, so you pick it back up when you're ready.
- **Events**: added `goal.parked_on_user` and `goal.park_skipped`.

I added 8 tests in `tests/server/goal-stop-hook-cap.test.ts`. They and the existing goal and ACP test suites pass (105 tests). The typecheck is clean for my files. It does show errors in `agent-runtime/manager.ts` and a few test files, which come from someone else's uncommitted work in progress, not from this change.

## Session fix
